```
def golomb_greedy(max_mark):
    marks=[0]
    diffs=set([0])? use boolean array length max_mark+1 for differences present.
    for x in range(1,max_mark+1):
       ok=True
       for a in marks:
          d=x-a
          if diff_exists[d]: ok=False; break
       if ok:
          add x; for a in marks: diff_exists[x-a]=True
          if len(marks)>=target: break
    return marks
```

### Candidate construction
Functions top-level, no closures. Need be careful no nested functions/lambdas. We'll define `make_uniform(n)`, `make_near_delta(n, eps)`, `make_two_level(n, rho, sign_seed)`, `make_golomb_weights(n, target_score?)`, `make_slepian(n, harmonics, rng)` etc.

**Golomb support**:
Implement `build_golomb_greedy(N)` returns marks up to N. Complexity O(m*N) with boolean diff array length N+1. We want m maybe max within N. We can build for final n maybe equal to N+1? Or for a given n, build support within n-1.
```
def golomb_greedy(N):
    marks=[0]
    used=np.zeros(N+1,dtype=bool)
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        ok=True
        for m in marks:
            d=x-m
            if used[d]: ok=False; break
        if ok:
            for m in marks:
                used[x-m]=True
            marks.append(x)
    return np.array(marks, dtype=int)
```
This could be O(sum x * m) if checking all x; for N=10000, m~100, 1e6, fine. But if we rebuild for many N, okay.
We can also build by target mark count with random restarts? For final n=6962, one call.
But if build for N=20000, m~200, 4M, okay.
For random support Sidon maybe use greedy random skip:
```
def random_sidon_support(N, m, rng): ...
```
Not necessary.

**Weights for support**:
- Equal: 1/m.
- Optimized for internal/off metric? Could use successive weight updates. If hidden off and near-delta trivial, equal not optimal; but near-delta candidate covers. If hidden mixed, optimize weights via coordinate ascent internal.
- For full metric, sparse invalid.

**Rudin-Shapiro signs**:
Generate RS signs recursively: sequence of length 2^k with a=00010101? Standard: start with [1]; iterate sequence = s, s flipped? For low autocorr. Simpler: Use Hadamard-matrices? Need signs ∑0 and low autocorr. We can generate random balanced signs and optimize? For applying as two-level, even random signs might help. Implement Rudin-Shapiro:
```
def rudin_shapiro(k):
    s=np.array([1])
    for _ in range(k):
       s=np.concatenate([s, -s[::-1]?]) # not sure
```
Rudin-Shapiro sequence: r_{2n}=r_n, r_{2n+1}=(-1)^n? Or recursive: starting [0,1,0,1], replace 0->0, 1->0? Let's implement known: RS sequence over bits 0/1: a_{2n}=a_n, a_{2n+1}=(-1)^n a_n? Not exactly. Simpler: Use **Mfoil**? We can generate a low autocorrelation binary sequence via greedy: start with +1, iteratively append a sign that minimizes current off-peak autocorrelation of the current sequence (or its convolution). For length 65536, O(n²) maybe heavy but okay for n=7000 if incremental O(n²)=49M manageable. We can also use balanced random signs.
Implement `low_autocorr_signs(n, rng)`:
- Initialize signs partly random or greedy.
- Greedy construction: signs array length n. For i from 0 to n-2? At each step choose append +1 or -1 to minimize max off autocorr of prefix? But final autocorr changes as length grows; a heuristic. O(n²). For n=6962, 24M okay. Or use known **Zadoff-Chu**? Need not.
Simpler: Use Fourier random phase to generate real sign? For nonnegative two-level, low autocorr random signs will have off peaks ~√n, while constant part envelope n-k; sign contribution ε√n, can reduce if ε large enough? Need ε*√n comparable to n? For n=7000, √n=84, ε up to1, reduction factor 84/7000 small (1%), not enough. Need deterministic Barker-like with autocorr O(log n)? Rudin-Shapiro peak O(√n) still. Two-level reduction modest. For LSE auto, p over d=0..n-1. Build K length 2n-1, K[n-1+d]=p[d] for d=0..n-1? But for d negative, abs(d) = -d, so K[n-1-d]=p[d]. Symmetric. If full auto v0=2p0, else p0. Good.

However LSE p over r_d (d included). For off auto, p0=0, v_d=p_d. For full, v0=2p0, v_d=p_d for d>0. Implement `lse_auto_score_grad(a,beta,include_zero)`:
- Compute auto r: Use FFT: M=1<<(2n-1).bit_length(); F=rfft(a,M); r=irfft(F*conj(F),M)[:n]. This gives linear autocorr? For zero-padded, r[d]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-d} a_i a_{i+d}. Should be correct.
- terms = r if include_zero else r[1:]
- If max<=0 return 0 grad.
- p_terms normalized. If include_zero: p_d length n, p[0]=p_terms[0], p[1:]=p_terms[1:]; else p[0]=0,p[1:]=p_terms.
- v = p.copy(); if include_zero: v[0] *= 2? Wait p0 used in gradient of r0 as 2a. In LSE over terms including r0, ∂LSE=∑ p_d ∂r_d, so v0=2p0, v_d=p_d. For off, p0=0.
- Build kernel K length 2n-1: K[n-1+d]=v[d] for d=0..n-1; for d=1..n-1 K[n-1-d]=v[d]. Then y=conv(a,K)[n-1:2n-1]. But conv length 3n-2, slice? Let's test simple n=3, v=[v0,v1,v2]. K indices 0..4: K[1]=v0, K[2]=v1, K[3]=v2, K[0]=v1, K[4]=v2. conv a*K:
t=1: a0*K1 = a0v0 => y0? plus a? t=n-1=1 for j=0: i=0 K1=v0 -> a0v0; i>0 none? y0 should v0 a0. good.
t=2 (j=1): i=0 a0K2=v1 a0; i=1 a1K1=v0 a1. y1 should v0a1+v1a0+v1a2? Wait v2? abs(2-1)=1 not 2, so v1a2 should appear? i=2: K[2-2=n-1-i=2-2=0], K0=v1; product a2v1. Good t=2 has i=0 K2, i=1K1, i=2K0. correct.
t=3 j=2: i=0 K3=v2, i=1 K2=v1, i=2K1=v0. good. So slice [n-1:2n-1] length n.
- grad = 2n*(y*S - 2L)/S³? Wait y already ∂L (gradient of LSE) since v includes r0 factor. Then score gradient = 2n*(y*S -2L)/S³ (no extra 2). Good.

For linear LSE, we can use previous method: p_full length 2n-1, rev_a convolution. Need P padded to M2=1<<(3n-2).bit_length(). `RA=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], M2); P=np.fft.rfft(p_full, M2); conv_res=np.fft.irfft(RA*P, M2)[n-1:2n-1]`; y=conv_res? Previous g=2*conv_res, and gradient formula 2n(g*S-2L)/S³. Equivalent y=conv_res and grad=2n*(2y*S -2L)/S³ = 4n*(y*S-L)/S³. Let's implement consistent: `h` where ∂c =2h. Use previous formula to avoid mistakes:
```
M2 = 1 << (3*n - 2).bit_length()
RA=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], M2)
P=np.fft.rfft(p_full, M2)
h=np.fft.irfft(RA*P,M2)[n-1:2n-1]
# h_j = ∑ a_i p_{i+j}
# ∂L = 2h
# score = 2n L/S²
grad = 2.0*n*(2.0*h*S - 2.0*L)/(S**3)
```
This equals 4n(h*S-L)/S³. For full/off.

For auto:
```
y = conv(a,K)[n-1:2n-1] # ∂L
grad = 2.0*n*(y*S - 2.0*L)/(S**3)
```
Need ensure y units: For off auto, ∂r_d has no factor 2, so y correct. For a single delta, r off 0; L=0.

For lse constant L offset: if beta large, L approximates max term; if terms near zero, stable. `beta` can be adaptive.

def safe_oracle(a):
    try:
       seq=[float(max(0.0,min(1000.0,x))) for x in a]
       if not seq: return np.inf
       if sum(seq)<0.01: return np.inf
       val=evaluate_sequence(seq)
       val=float(val)
       return val if np.isfinite(val) else np.inf
    except Exception:
       return np.inf
```

**fast_conv**:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    n=a.size
    m=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length() # Wait length full 2n-1, need power >=2n-1. Previous used 2n-2 bit_length. If n=2, full len3, 2n-2=2 bit_length=2, 1<<2=4 >=3; 1<<(3).bit_length() =1<<2=4 also. For n=1, full len1, 2n-2=0 bit_length0 =>1; 2n-1=1 bit_length1=>2, bigger okay. Use `1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()` safe.
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
    return np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)[:2*n-1]
```
For linear convolution no wrap if m>=2n-1. Good.

**fast_auto**:

r=np.fft.irfft(F*np.conj(F),m)[:n]
```
For zero-padded autocorr, is r[d]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-d} a_i a_{i+d}? The circular correlation includes also negative lags; positive d first n correct? Need test n=3 a=[a0,a1,a2]. F*conj(F) IFFT circular autocorr length m: r[d]=∑_{i} a_i a_{i+d mod m}. For d=1: i=0 a0a1, i=1 a1a2, i=2 a2*0 (since i+1 mod m =3 zero) => correct. d=2: a0a2, a1*0, a2*0 => correct. Good.

**lse_linear_score_grad** detail:
```
def lse_linear_score_grad(a,beta,include_zero):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).copy? no need but ensure finite.
    n=a.size
    c=fast_conv(a)
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-12:
       return 1e12, np.zeros(n)
    if include_zero:
       terms=c
    else:
       terms=c[1:]
       if terms.size==0: return 0., zeros
    cmax=float(terms.max())
    if cmax <= 0:
       return 0., zeros
    scaled=beta*(terms - cmax)
    maxscaled=float(scaled.max())
    e=np.exp(scaled - maxscaled)
    Z=e.sum()
    p=e/Z
    L=cmax + (maxscaled + float(np.log(Z)))/beta
    # p_full
    pfull=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    if include_zero: pfull=p
    else: pfull[1:]=p
    # h
    M2 = 1 << (3*n - 2).bit_length()  # for n=1, 1? 3*1-2=1 bit_length1 =>2? Actually need conv length n+ (2n-1)-1 =3n-2. If 3n-2=1, M2=2 okay.
    rev=a[::-1].copy()
    RA=np.fft.rfft(rev, M2)
    P=np.fft.rfft(pfull, M2)
    h=np.fft.irfft(RA*P, M2)[n-1:2*n-1]
    # numerical cleanup h real
    S2=S*S; S3=S2*S
    grad=(2.0*n/ S3) * (2.0*h*S - 2.0*L)
    score=2.0*n*L/S2
    # Add small sum reg? Maybe separate wrapper.
    return score, grad
```
Check gradient for full linear with uniform a=1, n large. c0=n, off max~n. LSE beta large L≈n. pfull almost at c0? For full, c0 unique max n, p0≈1. h_j = ∑ a_i p_{i+j}. If p concentrated at 0, h_j for j? p_0 only, h_j=a_j if i+j=0 => i=j=0? only j=0? That seems p0 only gradient of c0 =2a, not uniform. Score gradient would push a0? But c0=sum squares gradient=2a_j, not just j=0. Wait if p vector has p0=1, h_j=∑ a_i p_{i+j}; p only index 0, h_j nonzero only if i+j=0 for i in [0,n-1], j in [0,n-1] => i=0,j=0. That gives gradient only at index 0! Wrong! Because our formula h_j=∑_i a_i w_{i+j} for weights w over **sum index k**, and c0 corresponds k=0 only one term a0*a0. The gradient of c0=∑ all a_i² is not represented by weight at k=0? Let's re-evaluate c_k indices. For k=0, c0=∑_{i=0}^0? Now our generic LSE linear algorithm will be the core; we need improve over previous. The previous code optimized full linear LSE but may get stuck in local minima. We can use insights: **Sparse support / combinatorial design**. We should generate supports and optimize weights. Also use iterative reweighting as in state-of-art.

Let's design a stronger search specifically for linear convolution.

Objective: minimize `F(a)=2n max(c)/S²`, c=conv(a,a). We can normalize S=1. We can consider **active set / LP**: Given current a (or target M), the set of peaks are lags k where c_k near M. To improve, we want find b≥0 with small convolution under current weights? The state LP: for fixed f, maximize sum b subject to ||f*b||∞≤M. This is a way to produce a new sequence g that is feasible against current peaks? Actually M=||f*f||∞. If b is feasible with f*b≤M, then for normalization same, maybe g has lower max? It's an outer approximation.

We can implement a simpler **weight optimization on random/multiple supports** using **scipy minimize** with objective LSE over convolution. Starting from many sparse / smooth initializations and using L-BFGS can improve.

But gradients on full n with LSE may be expensive but okay. The previous approach used LSE and GS; need do something different:
- Use **active-set QP** or **multiplicative updates** for nonnegative second-order cone? 
- Use **sparse support generation via greedy weak Sidon / modular constructions**, then **weight optimization** on support via small-dimensional convex (actually load-minimizing) problem. Let's explore weight optimization for fixed support.

For fixed support A (size m) and values w≥0, c_s = ∑_{i,j: i+j=s} w_i w_j. The objective (S=1) minimize max_s c_s. This is a nonconvex quadratic. But it can be solved by **alternating linearization**: fix current w, let loads L_s. To reduce max, we can formulate a semidefinite? For small m, use gradient LSE. Could also use **successive max-load reduction**: weights update w_i ← w_i / sqrt(exp(β load contributions))? This resembles minimizing maximum of quadratic via power iteration. A multiplicative water-filling:
For normalized S=1, define effective load on pair (i,j) if sum s active. Gradient of max load = sum over active s of partner weights (ordered). We can do mirror descent: w_i ← w_i * exp(-η density_i) where density_i = ∑_{active s} ∇c_s/w? This is similar to previous peak-targeted refinement. But L-BFGS is fine.

We can improve weight optimization with **active set LP** as in state-of-art:
Given current weights w and peak set P (LSE top sums), the subproblem minimize `max_{s∈P} c_s`? The LP in prompt: fix f and maximize sum g subject to f*g≤M. That's for linear objective in g given f: `n_k = ∑_i f_i g_{k-i}`. If we fix f = current sqrt(a)? Wait self-convolution a*a is quadratic; if we set f = sqrt? Not linear. But for alternating, we can fix one side: represent a_i? If we set a as variable on one side and b on other? Not same. However given f and find g such that f*g has small max and sum large. If we update a ← (f+g)/2? The prompt does f←(1-t)f+tg. This is a two-sided alternating minimization for the product of two independent sequences? But objective is f*f, not f*g. If we set f*g as cross-convolution; minimizing f*f and f*g? If g approximates f, controlling f*g controls f*f. The LP maximizes L1 of g with f*g≤M. This can be solved via scipy linear programming for m support? Could provide strong moves.

Let's exploit LP for support:
- Fix current weight vector f (nonnegative, sum=1). Let target M = current max load (or slightly smaller times sum?). We want maximize ∑ g_j subject to g_j≥0 and (f*g)_k ≤ M * S_f S_g? Since score independent? If we keep sums S_f=S_g=1, constraint f*g ≤ M (where M is load). Then rescale g to sum=1. If the feasible sum is S_g0, then the best g has sum S_g0; normalizing to sum1 divides, so new loads (g*g?)? The LP ensures cross loads f*g low; it may help reduce self loads by mixing. The state algorithm exactly uses this. We can implement for support of size m: variables g length n? If using full n, too big. But we can restrict g to same support variables (m). Constraint f_i g_{k-i} ≤ M: for each sum k, ∑_{i,j: p_i+p_j=k} f_i g_j ≤ M. This is a linear program in g with A_{k,j}=∑_i f_i δ_{p_i+p_j=k}. 2n constraints (14000), m vars (≤300). Could solve with scipy.optimize.linprog? Method highspy not available; cvxpy with CBC/ECOS? Could be slow but m small. Or use simple projected gradient for LP: maximize sum g s.t. A g ≤ M, g≥0. This is a feasibility/linear program; we can solve via `scipy.optimize.linprog` (HiGHS) maybe okay for 300 vars, 14000 constraints? Could be heavy but occasional. Alternatively, use dual / iterative: Since constraints sparse, we can use a **water-filling / multiplicative gap** algorithm: for fixed A,M, maximize sum g. This is a linear program; can solve by linear programming or by projection onto convex set? The feasible set {Ag≤M,g≥0}. We want point with max sum. Equivalent to find g with all constraints. We can use linear programming if we import scipy; HiGHS efficient for 14k×300? 4.2M constraints? Actually each j couples to about m sums? A dense? For each sum k, A_{k,j}=∑_i f_i [p_i+p_j=k]. For random support, each (i,j) pair one sum; A has m² nonzeros distributed among 2n, so average m²/(2n) ~1-2; sparse. SciPy linprog may not handle sparse well. We can avoid.

But weight L-BFGS might be enough.

**Direct construction using modular sequences**:
Maybe known **Levenshtein sequences** or **Mersenne** have low autocorrelation; for sums, use perfect difference sets in cyclic group of size q^2-1. Let's examine modular construction for **convolution sums**: If A is a difference set in Z_v, then the number of representations of a residue as difference is λ. But sums? If we map elements by doubling? In cyclic group, sum distribution of A is convolution of indicator with itself. A set whose cyclic convolution is flat (including wrap) is a **Bose-Chowla** or **perfect difference set under sums**? We can choose A as a **perfect difference set** and then place each element in a linearspread using centering. For cyclic convolution: *A*A modulo v has counts: c_0=k, c_s=λ for s≠0 if A is a (v,k,λ) difference set? Actually difference set controls autocorrelation (sums with -A), not A*A. But if we take A symmetric (closed under negation?) maybe sums behave. There are **planar functions** giving perfect nonlinear: A = graph of f: {(x, f(x))} in F_q². Then each nonzero vector has λ representations as difference, but sums of two graph points? The sum (x1+ x2, f(x1)+f(x2)) not constant. If use character sums? Too deep.

Greedy support + weight optimization more reliable.

### Weight optimization on full sequence vs support
Support weight optimization gives exact gradient for fixed support. But it may get stuck due support. We can combine with full LSE L-BFGS from best dense/support candidate to allow support adaptation: full L-BFGS can create new nonzeros and adjust all. Run after support optimization.

Full LSE for linear convolution (not auto) same as previous `lse_linear_score_grad`. We'll use only linear. This is simpler.

### Improving over previous algorithm
Previous had full LSE L-BFGS, GS, iterative reweighting, peak-targeted. We can do:
1. **Calibrate/generate supports** with controlled sum multiplicity (new).
2. **Support weight optimization via exact LSE/FFT** (new, faster, focused).
3. **Full LSE L-BFGS from best supports and parent** with multiplicative gap/LP? (previous but improved starts).
4. **Iterative reweighting specialized**: For best candidate, compute active peak weights p_s (LSE or uniform over top), solve a **proximal quadratic subproblem** on weights/support: minimize ∑ p_s c_s + regularizer. On support, this is quadratic `w^T P w` with P_{ij}=p_{s_i+s_j}. We can minimize this quadratic by a few projected gradient/L-BFGS; equivalently LSE with high beta. Already.
5. **Support local search**: Replace support elements to reduce equal-weight or optimized score. New.

This strategy likely beats.

Let's implement support generation and optimizer carefully.

### Support generator details
```
def random_support(n, m, rng):
    return rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)

def greedy_support(n, m, B=200, max_hard=10, rng=None, seed=0):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    counts=np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=np.int32)
    used=np.zeros(n, bool)
    existing=np.empty(0, dtype=int)
    # start with one random position maybe 0
    first = int(rng.integers(n))
    used[first]=True; existing=np.array([first])
    # update loop count
    counts[2*first] +=1
    for step in range(1,m):
       if existing.size==0: ...
       # candidate pool: random unused positions. We can also bias to positions near gaps: random uniform ok.
       # To avoid O(n) finding unused, just sample with rejection.
       cands=[]
       while len(cands)<B:
          x=rng.integers(n)
          if not used[x]: cands.append(x)
       cands=np.array(cands)
       # compute sums with existing
       sums = cands[:,None] + existing[None,:]  # int
       new_counts = counts[sums] + 2
       loop_counts = counts[2*cands] + 1
       max_new = np.maximum(new_counts.max(axis=1), loop_counts)
       # Cost: max then sum top maybe
       # For tie, compute squared sum of new_counts along existing plus loop
       l2 = np.sum(new_counts.astype(np.float64)**2, axis=1) + loop_counts.astype(float)**2
       # Also maybe mean
       order = np.lexsort((l2, max_new)) # primary max_new, secondary l2. But max_new int; lexsort okay.
       # choose maybe several candidates from top to stochastic? choose best.
       x=int(cands[order[0]])
       # if max_new > max_hard and step large? accept still; could skip? For quality, if > hard, maybe try until no better; but need fill.
       used[x]=True
       existing=np.append(existing,x)
       counts[x+x] +=1
       # update counts for x+existing excluding x? existing before adding has no x; do np.add.at(counts, existing + x, 2)
       np.add.at(counts, existing + x, 2) # x+existing
       # existing has old only; okay
    return existing
```
Potential issue: `np.add.at` after `existing` append? Use old existing before append. Save `old_existing=existing`, then update, then append.
Costs: For m=250,B=300, each step sums 75k, l2 sum 75k, total ~2M, fine. But creating B*m arrays per step maybe memory okay (B=200,m=150=30k). We can reduce B=120 for speed. Use `rng.choice` many.

Support selection score:
```
def support_equal_score(supp,n):
    m=supp.size
    sums = supp[:,None] + supp[None,:].ravel()
    counts = np.bincount(sums, minlength=2*n-1)
    maxc=counts.max()
    return 2.0*n*maxc/(m*m), counts.max(), counts
```

**Local improve implementation details**:
```
def refine_support_counts(supp, n, iters=1000, rng=None, temp=0.1):
    supp=np.array(supp,dtype=int).copy()
    m=supp.size
    counts=np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=np.int64)
    # build counts: sums=supp[:,None]+supp[None,:]; np.add.at? use bincount length 2n-1 from ravel
    sums=supp[:,None]+supp[None,:]
    counts=np.bincount(sums.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1).astype(np.int64)
    used=np.zeros(n,dtype=bool); used[supp]=True
    sumsq=int(np.sum(counts*counts))
    maxc=int(counts.max())
    T=temp
    for it in range(iters):
       idx=int(rng.integers(m))
       # generate candidates maybe 20 to find improvement
       found=False
       old=supp[idx]
       others = maybe precompute list without idx? Use boolean mask each iter expensive. We can create `others = np.delete(supp, idx)` when needed. m small; deletion O(m). okay.
       # Remove old from counts and update sumsq/maxc? maintain counts_without.
       # old contributions: inc array 2 for others +1 for self. But using supp with idx.
       sums_old = old + supp  # includes old+old at idx, old+others
       inc_old = np.full(m,2,dtype=np.int64); inc_old[idx]=1
       # update sumsq: for each s, c=counts[s], delta = - (2*c*inc + inc²?) Wait inc may multiple at same sum? old+supp unique (since supp unique), self distinct. sumsq_new = sumsq - Σ ( (c)^2 - (c-inc)^2 ) = sumsq - Σ(2c inc - inc²)
       vals=counts[sums_old]
       # If duplicate sums? no because old+supp unique as supp unique. self old+old may coincide with old+y if y=old only; no.
       sumsq_no = sumsq - int(np.sum(2*vals*inc_old - inc_old*inc_old))
       np.add.at(counts, sums_old, -inc_old)
       old_max_no = int(counts.max())  # O(n) each iter if every iter; 1000*14000=14M okay.
       best = None
       # candidate pool: maybe 30 random unused
       cands=[]; for _ in range(30): x=...; if not used[x]
       for x in cands:
          sums_new = x + others
          vals_new = counts[sums_new]
          inc_new = np.full(len(others),2)
          loop_val = counts[2*x]
          # sumsq if add new to counts_without:
          delta_sq = int(np.sum(2*vals_new*2 - 2*2)) + (2*loop_val*1 -1)
          sumsq_new = sumsq_no + delta_sq
          max_new = max(old_max_no, counts_new affected max)
          # affected max: np.max(np.maximum(sums_new vals+2, loop_val+1)) if others nonempty
          ...
          compare (max_new,sumsq_new) to (best)
       if best:
          # accept: counts already without old; apply new
          x=best_x
          sums_new=x+others; vals=counts[sums_new]; sumsq_new=...; np.add.at(counts,sums_new,2); counts[2*x]+=1; used[old]=False; used[x]=True; supp[idx]=x; maxc=best_max; sumsq=best_sumsq; current_no? We need counts now full. But counts currently without old. After accept, it's full. For next iteration, fine.
          found=True
          # cooling T? We'll only accept non-increasing; no temp needed. Use random candidate pool with stochastic alternative: if best equal score? accept with some probability.
       else:
          # restore old
          vals=counts[sums_old]
          # counts currently without old; add old: delta_sq = (c+inc)^2 - c^2 =2c inc+inc²
          np.add.at(counts,sums_old,inc_old)
          sumsq = sumsq_no + int(np.sum(2*vals*inc_old + inc_old*inc_old))
          maxc = int(counts.max())
       # If equal non-increasing? If no improvement, keep.
    return supp, counts, int(counts.max()), sumsq
```
Need ensure `old_max_no` computed after removal; `counts.max()` each iter expensive but okay? 40*1000*14000 ~560M operations (C max?) maybe heavy. We can avoid max every iter by noting removal can only decrease or leave max. We can maintain current max after removal approximately: if old contributions did not contain the unique maximum, old_max_no=maxc; if they did, recompute. But simpler maybe use `np.max(counts[np.nonzero?]`? Could be slow.
Alternative: Do not maintain max during candidate evaluation exactly; just use `sumsq` and recompute equal score after local search. For acceptance, we can test candidate by temporarily updating? Too slow. We can accept based on pair `(new_max, new_sumsq)` where `new_max` can be upper-bounded by `old_max_no`. Need `old_max_no`.    # remove old

   np.add.at(counts, old+supp, -inc_old)

   best=None
   others = np.delete(supp, idx)

      affected = cand + others
      if affected.size: vals=counts[affected]; candidate_max1=int(vals.max()+2)
      else candidate_max1=0
      loop_val=counts[2*cand]
      cand_max=max(max_no, candidate_max1, loop_val+1)
      # sumsq inc
      inc_sq = (4*vals.sum() + 4*len(affected)) + (2*loop_val + 1)
      cand_sumsq=sumsq_no + int(inc_sq)
      if best is None or cand_max<best_max or (== and cand_sumsq<best_sumsq): best=...

      # apply best to counts (currently without old)
      affected=best_x+others; np.add.at(counts, affected,2); counts[2*best_x]+=1
      used[old]=False; used[best_x]=True; supp[idx]=best_x
      maxc=best_max; sumsq=best_sumsq
   else:
      # restore old
      np.add.at(counts, old+supp, inc_old)
      # maxc,sumsq unchanged (counts full)
```

`run_support_lbfgs` sets `_SUPP_N` and `_SUMS`, `_SUPP_BETA`. It calls `minimize(support_lbfgs_fun, x0, bounds=...)`. The objective uses `_SUMPS` from global. Need ensure if x length changes, okay.

`support_lbfgs_fun` details with `np.bincount`:
```
def support_lbfgs_fun(x):
    w=np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    m=w.size
    S=float(np.sum(w))
    if S < 1e-12:
       return 1e12, np.zeros(m)
    outer = w[:,None] * w[None,:]
    vals = outer.ravel()
    c = np.bincount(_SUMS.ravel(), weights=vals, minlength=2*_SUPP_N-1)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
       return 0.0, np.zeros(m)
    scaled = _SUPP_BETA * (c - cmax)
    # c length maybe 20000, okay
    maxs = float(scaled.max())
    e = np.exp(scaled - maxs)
    Z = float(e.sum())
    p = e / Z
    L = cmax + (maxs + np.log(Z)) / _SUPP_BETA
    D = p[_SUMS]
    grad_L = 2.0 * (D @ w)
    score = 2.0 * _SUPP_N * L / (S*S)
    grad = (2.0 * _SUPP_N / (S**3)) * (grad_L * S - 2.0 * L)
    # small sum regularization
    reg = 1e-10 * (S - 1.0) ** 2
    grad = grad + 2e-10 * (S - 1.0)
    # ensure finite
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)): grad = np.nan_to_num(grad)
    return score + reg, grad
```
Potential performance: `D = p[_SUMS]` creates m×m; `D @ w` okay. For many evals, outer product and bincount each time. Could precompute `_SUMS_RAVEL` to avoid ravel global? Store `_SUMS_R = _SUMS.ravel()`. In global, set both. Use `_SUMS_R` in bincount and `D = p[_SUMS]` uses 2D.
`np.bincount` weights length m²; if m=0? not.
If m large 350, m²=122k, 5000 evals 610M float ops, maybe too slow. Limit m<=250 for weight L-BFGS, or maxfun lower. Cap support optimizer m <= 220? But target may need 250. Let's set m_cap=260. For m=260, 67k; maxfun 1200 maybe 80M, okay. Use time estimates.

`support_weight_internal_score(supp,w,n)`: same as objective but no reg, return exact max score.

**Full L-BFGS global**:
```
def full_lbfgs_fun(x):
   f,g = lse_linear_score_grad(x, _FULL_BETA)
   S=float(np.sum(x))
   reg=1e-10*(S-1.0)**2
   grad=g + 2e-10*(S-1.0)
   return f+reg, grad
```

### Direct internal score for support weights
`support_weight_score`:
```
def support_weight_score(supp,w,n):
   S=w.sum(); if S<... inf
   outer=w[:,None]*w[None,:]
   c=np.bincount((supp[:,None]+supp[None,:]).ravel(), weights=outer.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1)
   return 2*n*float(c.max())/(S*S)
```
Use same as objective but no reg.

### Testing mathematical support equal score
If uniform dense length n, score 2. Support equal weights m=100 with max ordered count C maybe score=2n*C/m². For n=6962, m=136, C=2 ->1.507 (target close). Greedy supports may find C=3/4; weight optimization can reduce. Good.

### State-of-art LP implementation possibility
Maybe we can implement an LP-like step for support weights to improve further. Let's see if easy with `scipy.optimize.linprog` using support variables. Current f weights, target M maybe current max load * (1+epsilon). We want maximize sum g s.t. A g ≤ M, g≥0. Then normalize g and mix. This may lead to strong global moves.
For support m≤200, constraints 2n (14000). But we can reduce constraints to only *active* sums? The state uses all lags with bound M. For upper bound, need all. However we can represent A sparse: A_{k,j}=∑_i f_i δ_{p_i+p_j=k}. For each j, A_j has nonzero entries at sums p_j+p_i over i (m entries). Thus sparse matrix size (2n-1)×m with m² nonzeros. `scipy.sparse` and `linprog` highest may handle? 14000×200, 40k nonzeros, objective maximize sum g. Feasible. But solving LP multiple times may be slow; HiGHS can solve maybe seconds. We can try optional if time:
- Build sparse matrix from lists: for each sum k, rows; for each j, col list of (row, value). Use `scipy.sparse.coo_matrix`. Then `linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=M*(S_f? constraints scale), bnd=(0,inf))`. But nvariables 200, rows 14000, dense conversion by HiGHS maybe. Could be too slow.
- Alternative use projected gradient on LP constraints: maximize sum g s.t. A g ≤ M. We can do: initialize g; iteratively `g += step*(1 - 2*? )`? Not robust.
Maybe skip LP; LSE enough.

### Multiplicative updates for support weights
Could be beneficial and cheap. Implement `support_multiplicative(supp,w,n,iters=50,beta=100)`:
```
for it:
  c,p,L,D,grad_L = compute support LSE (like objective)
  G = D @ w  # unnormalized gradient load for each weight (not divided S)
  # To reduce weights contributing to high loads: w2 = w / (G/S + eps)
  t = 0.5*(1 - it/iters)+0.1
  w_new = w * (0.5 -? )
```
A known power iteration for minimizing max of quadratic on simplex: `w_i <- w_i / (sum_j p_{ij} w_j + eps)`, normalize. Since D approx active load distribution. We can blend and accept if internal score improves.
Implement compute function `support_lse_parts(supp,w,n,beta)` top-level returning c,p,L,D? It can be used in multiplicative and optional. But to keep code simpler, maybe skip.

def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```

`clean_array(a, to_sum=1.0)`:
```
def clean_array(a, to_sum=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if a.size==0: return np.array([1.0])
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S<0.01:
       a=np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    else:
       a=a/S*to_sum
    return a
```
But for score, scale irrelevant; return sum 1.

`make_uniform(n): return np.ones(n)/n`

def load_parent_array():
    try:
       h=height_sequence_1
       if h is None: return None
       a=np.asarray(h,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
       if a.size<10: return None
       S=a.sum()
       if S<0.01: return None
       return a/S
    except Exception: return None
```

`greedy_support(n,m,rng)`:
Need handle m>n. `m=min(m,n)`. Use int.
Pseudo with existing as np.array. For candidate evaluation, if existing size 0? Start first random/0. For step, candidate pool B. Use `np.lexsort`, which expects keys last primary? `np.lexsort((l2, max_new))` sorts by max_new primary then l2. Good. If m=1 returns [first]. For updating counts, use `np.add.at`.
Use `np.int64` for positions/sums. If n large, sums up to 2n-2.
Candidate pool size B: set dynamic `B = min(200, max(50, 2*n//m?))`. For speed B=120.
```
def greedy_support(n,m,rng):
   n=int(n); m=min(int(m),n)
   if m<=0: return np.array([],dtype=np.int64)
   used=np.zeros(n,dtype=bool)
   counts=np.zeros(2*n-1,dtype=np.int64)
   first=int(rng.integers(n))
   used[first]=True
   existing=np.array([first],dtype=np.int64)
   counts[2*first] +=1
   for _ in range(m-1):
      B=150
      cands=[]
      while len(cands)<B:
         x=int(rng.integers(n))
         if not used[x]: cands.append(x)
      cands=np.array(cands,dtype=np.int64)
      if existing.size==0: ...
      sums=cands[:,None]+existing[None,:]
      new=counts[sums]+2
      loop=counts[2*cands]+1
      max_new=np.maximum(new.max(axis=1),loop)
      # Use float l2
      l2=np.sum(new.astype(np.float64)*new.astype(np.float64),axis=1) + loop.astype(np.float64)*loop.astype(np.float64)
      order=np.lexsort((l2,max_new))
      x=int(cands[order[0]])
      # if max_new[order[0]] > current_max*? maybe still accept
      # update
      used[x]=True
      # update counts with old existing
      np.add.at(counts, existing + x, 2)
      counts[2*x] +=1
      existing=np.append(existing,x)
   return existing
```
`np.lexsort` with max_new int and l2 float okay. Need ensure if B small. If no unused (m=n), while infinite? If m close n, candidate while could loop. But m/n <=~3/√n? For n=1024,m=256, unused many. If m==n, we can just return arange. At top: if m>=n: return np.arange(n,dtype=np.int64).

`refine_support_counts` as simple:
```
def refine_support_counts(supp,n,iters=300,cand_pool=20,rng=None):
   supp=np.array(supp,dtype=np.int64).copy()
   m=supp.size
   if m==0: return supp, counts,0,0
   used=np.zeros(n,dtype=bool); used[supp]=True
   sums=supp[:,None]+supp[None,:]
   counts=np.bincount(sums.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1).astype(np.int64)
   maxc=int(counts.max()); sumsq=int(np.dot(counts,counts))
   temp? We'll only accept improvements.
   for it in range(iters):
      idx=int(rng.integers(m))
      old=supp[idx]
      # remove old
      inc_old=np.full(m,2,dtype=np.int64); inc_old[idx]=1
      old_sums=old+supp
      np.add.at(counts, old_sums, -inc_old)
      max_no=int(counts.max())
      sumsq_no=int(np.dot(counts,counts))
      others=np.delete(supp, idx)
      best_x=-1; best_max=maxc; best_sumsq=sumsq  # compare to original full
      # generate candidates
      cands=[]
      while len(cands)<cand_pool:
         x=int(rng.integers(n))
         if not used[x] and int(x)!=old: cands.append(x)
      if others.size>0:
         # vectorized candidate evaluation: cands shape Cx1, others 1xM -> CxM
         # To limit memory, C=20,M≤260 ->5200 fine.
         all_sums = np.array(cands,dtype=np.int64)[:,None] + others[None,:]
         vals = counts[all_sums]
         maxs_vals = vals.max(axis=1) + 2
         loop_sums = 2*np.array(cands)
         loop_vals=counts[loop_sums]
         cand_max = np.maximum(np.maximum(max_no, maxs_vals), loop_vals + 1)
         # sumsq increments: for each row sum over (vals+2)^2 - vals^2 = 4*vals+4
         sq_inc = np.sum(4*vals.astype(np.int64) + 4, axis=1) + (2*loop_vals + 1)
         cand_sumsq = sumsq_no + sq_inc
         # choose best, with tie random
         valid = (cand_max < best_max) | ((cand_max == best_max) & (cand_sumsq < best_sumsq))
         if np.any(valid):
             # choose random among valid
             choices=np.where(valid)[0]
             pick=int(rng.integers(choices.size))
             best_idx=choices[pick]
             best_x=cands[best_idx]; best_max=int(cand_max[best_idx]); best_sumsq=int(cand_sumsq[best_idx])
      else:
         # no others (m=1) maybe move to any? not useful
      if best_x != -1:
         # apply best: counts currently without old
         if others.size>0:
            np.add.at(counts, best_x + others, 2)
         counts[2*best_x] +=1
         used[old]=False; used[best_x]=True
         supp[idx]=best_x
         maxc=best_max; sumsq=best_sumsq
      else:
         # restore old
         np.add.at(counts, old_sums, inc_old)
         # maxc,sumsq should remain original
      if (it+1)%100==0:
         # recompute to correct drift
         maxc=int(counts.max()); sumsq=int(np.dot(counts,counts))
   # final recompute counts from supp for safety
   sums=supp[:,None]+supp[None,:]
   counts=np.bincount(sums.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1).astype(np.int64)
   maxc=int(counts.max()); sumsq=int(np.dot(counts,counts))
   return supp, counts, maxc, sumsq
```
Potential bug: When accept, `used[old]=False`; but `cands` chosen excludes used positions; after accept, best_x not used before, okay. The `others` array used for candidate evaluation and apply is same. If best_x equals some old? candidates exclude used, so no. If old has been removed from used? We didn't set used[old]=False while evaluating candidates, so `used` still True for old, and candidates exclude old (correct because old position can be reused? Moving a mark back to same position is unnecessary; exclude). After accept, set used old False, best True. Good. The `counts` during candidate eval do not include old; `used` includes old. Fine.
Potential bug: `np.add.at(counts, old_sums, -inc_old)` where `old_sums=old+supp` includes old+old and old+others; inc_old has 1 at idx, 2 for others. This exactly removes ordered pairs with old. Good.
Restoring after reject: counts without old; add old again. Good.
If `cand_pool` fails to find unused (m near n), while infinite. We cap; if m/n >0.9, skip. For our m small, okay. But safe: track attempts.
```
attempts=0
while len(cands)<cand_pool and attempts<cand_pool*20: attempts+=1; ...
if no cands: break
```
No need for metric max_hard. Good.

`support_equal_score`:
```
def support_equal_score(supp,n):
   m=supp.size
   if m<=0: return np.inf,0
   sums=(supp[:,None]+supp[None,:]).ravel()
   counts=np.bincount(sums, minlength=2*n-1)
   maxc=int(counts.max())
   sc=2.0*n*maxc/(m*m)
   return sc,maxc
```

`candidate_m_sizes(n)`:
```
def candidate_m_sizes(n):
   base=float(np.sqrt(n))
   raw=[]
   for c in [0.8,1.0,1.2,1.4,1.6,1.8,2.0,2.3,2.6,3.0]: raw.append(int(c*base+0.5))
   # Also add specific around target equal: int(np.sqrt(8.0*n/1.5)) maybe
   raw.append(int(np.sqrt(8.0*n/1.5)+0.5))
   raw.append(int(np.sqrt(12.0*n/1.5)+0.5))
   ms=set()
   for x in raw:
      x=max(24,min(260,int(x)))
      ms.add(x)
   return sorted(ms)
```
For n=6962 base83, m list [66,83,100,117,132,149,166,191,216,249,136,168]. 12 sizes; maybe too many. Limit to 8: choose around 120-240. Could just take last 8? We want variety. In search, if time limited, m_list = candidate_m_sizes(n); if len>6, subset every other.
```
ms = candidate_m_sizes(n)
if len(ms)>6: ms = ms[::2] + [ms[-1]]
```

`search_support_candidates(n, stop_time, rng, max_supports=10, max_opt=4)` details:
```
def search_support_candidates(n, stop_time, rng, max_opt=4):
   ms=candidate_m_sizes(n)
   if len(ms)>6: ms=ms[::2]
   pool=[]
   for idx,m in enumerate(ms):
       if time.monotonic() > stop_time: break
       # greedy 2 or 3
       for rep in range(2):
          if time... break
          supp=greedy_support(n,m,rng)
          pool.append(supp)
       supp=rand.choice...
       pool.append(supp)
   # evaluate and keep all scored
   scored=[]
   for supp in pool:
      if supp.size<20: continue
      sc,maxc=support_equal_score(supp,n)
      scored.append((sc,maxc,supp))
   if not scored: return []
   scores=np.array([x[0] for x in scored])
   order=np.argsort(scores)
   R=min(8,len(scored))
   top=[scored[i] for i in order[:R]]
   # refine top
   refined=[]
   for sc,maxc,supp in top:
      if time.monotonic() > stop_time: break
      supp_ref,_,maxc_ref,_=refine_support_counts(supp,n,iters=300,cand_pool=20,rng=rng)
      sc_ref=2.0*n*maxc_ref/(supp_ref.size**2)
      refined.append((sc_ref,supp_ref))
   # combine original top and refined and sort by score; include unique by rounding? skip unique
   opts=top+refined
   oscores=np.array([x[0] for x in opts])
   oorder=np.argsort(oscores)
   opt_top=[opts[i] for i in oorder[:min(max_opt*2,len(opts))]]
   candidates=[]
   seen=[] # frozenset? support tuple maybe large but m small; can use bytes? Not needed.
   for sc,supp in opt_top:
      if time.monotonic() > stop_time - 30: break
      if len(candidates)>=max_opt: break
      # maybe skip if same set as previous:
      key=tuple(np.sort(supp).tolist())
      if key in seen: continue
      seen.append(key)
      w=np.full(supp.size,1.0/supp.size)
      best_w=w; best_sc=np.inf
      m=supp.size
      maxfun_base = 3000 if m<=100 else 1800 if m<=180 else 900
      for beta in [20.0,100.0,500.0,2000.0]:
         if time.monotonic() > stop_time: break
         w=run_support_lbfgs(w,supp,n,beta,maxiter=800,maxfun=maxfun_base)
         scw=support_weight_score(supp,w,n)
         if scw<best_sc:
            best_sc=scw; best_w=w.copy()
      a=support_to_array(supp,best_w,n)
      candidates.append((best_sc,a,supp,best_w.copy()))
   return candidates
```

### Full score internal and LSE gradient correctness with full c
Let's implement `lse_linear_score_grad(a,beta)` top-level:
```
def lse_linear_score_grad(a,beta):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    c=fast_conv(a)
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-8:
       return 1e12, np.zeros(n)
    cmax=float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
       return 0.0, np.zeros(n)
    scaled=beta*(c-cmax)
    maxs=float(scaled.max())
    e=np.exp(scaled-maxs)
    Z=float(e.sum())
    p=e/Z
    L=cmax+(maxs+np.log(Z))/beta
    M2=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    rev=a[::-1].copy()
    RA=np.fft.rfft(rev,M2)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,M2)
    h=np.fft.irfft(RA*P,M2)[n-1:2*n-1]
    S2=S*S; S3=S2*S
    score=2.0*n*L/S2
    grad=(2.0*n/S3)*(2.0*h*S - 2.0*L)
    grad=np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return float(score), grad
```
Check if cmax maybe zero; okay.
`fast_conv` returns real maybe tiny negative; but max likely nonnegative. For LSE, negative c terms negligible; but if all zeros? If a has sum positive, some c positive. `cmax` positive. `scaled` for negative c can be very negative; exp underflow fine. `np.log(Z)` if Z=0? At least max term exp(0)=1, Z>0.

`score_full_internal`:
```
def score_full_internal(a):
   a=np.asarray(a)
   n=a.size; S=float(a.sum())
   if S<1e-8: return np.inf
   c=fast_conv(a); mx=float(c.max())
   return float(2.0*n*mx/(S*S))
```

def run_support_lbfgs(w0, supp, n, beta, maxiter, maxfun):
   w0=np.asarray(w0,dtype=np.float64).copy()
   S=float(w0.sum())
   if S<1e-8 or not np.any(w0>0): w0=np.full(len(w0),1.0/len(w0))
   else: w0/=S
   set_support_global(supp,n,beta)
   bounds=[(0.0,1000.0)]*len(w0)
   res=minimize(support_lbfgs_fun,w0,bounds=bounds,method='L-BFGS-B',options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxfun,'ftol':1e-15,'gtol':1e-12})
   w=np.nan_to_num(res.x,0,0,0) maybe `np.nan_to_num(res.x)`
   w=np.clip(w,0,1000); S=w.sum(); if S<... return w0; else w/=S
   return w
```

Top-level:
```
def support_heuristic_weights(supp,n,iters=30):
   m=supp.size
   w=np.full(m,1.0/m)
   sums=supp[:,None]+supp[None,:]
   for _ in range(iters):
      outer=w[:,None]*w[None,:]
      cnt=np.bincount(sums.ravel(), weights=outer.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1)
      tot=cnt.sum()
      if tot<=0: break
      p=cnt/tot
      D=p[sums]
      G=D@w
      G=np.maximum(G,1e-12)
      w2=w/G
      S2=w2.sum()
      if S2<...: break
      w2/=S2
      w=0.5*w+0.5*w2
      # if any huge clip? w=clip(w,1e-12,None); w/=w.sum()
   return w
```

`normalize_support`:
```
def normalize_support(supp):
   supp=np.asarray(supp,dtype=np.int64).ravel()
   if supp.size==0: return supp,1
   mn=int(supp.min()); mx=int(supp.max())
   supp=supp-mn
   # sort
   supp=np.sort(supp)
   # remove duplicates (if any)
   if supp.size>1:
      supp=np.unique(supp)
   return supp, int(supp.max())+1 if supp.size else 1
```
Need no lambda.

```
def trim_small(a, eps=1e-14):
   if a.size==0: return a
   mask=a>eps
   if not mask.any(): return a
   idx=np.where(mask)[0]
   first=int(idx[0]); last=int(idx[-1])
   if first>0 or last<a.size-1:
       a=a[first:last+1]
   return a
```

### Random smooth starts for full L-BFGS
Add candidates like cosine, random Fourier to complete. `make_fourier_init(n,rng)`:
```
def make_fourier_init(n,rng):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
   acc=np.ones(n)
   for k in range(1,12):
      amp=rng.uniform(0.05,0.3)
      phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
      freq=rng.integers(1,8)*k # maybe
      acc += amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x+phase)
   acc=np.maximum(acc,1e-3)
   return clean_array(acc)
```
No lambda. This can provide diverse full starts. Generate 3.

### Top-level function `support_lheuristic_weights` uses `p=cnt/tot`; if tot=0, break. `D @ w` okay.m maybe large; if score heuristic sucks, fallback equal. If heuristic weights all zero due collapse, check sum.

```
def support_heuristic_weights(supp,n,iters=30):
    m=supp.size
    if m<=0: return None
    w=np.full(m,1.0/m)
    sums=supp[:,None]+supp[None,:]
    for _ in range(iters):
       outer=w[:,None]*w[None,:]
       cnt=np.bincount(sums.ravel(), weights=outer.ravel(), minlength=2*n-1)
       tot=float(cnt.sum())
       if tot<1e-300: break
       p=cnt/tot
       D=p[sums]
       G=(D@w)+1e-12
       w2=w/G
       s2=float(w2.sum())
       if s2<1e-300: break
       w2/=s2
       w=0.5*w+0.5*w2
       w=np.clip(w,1e-12,None)
       s=float(w.sum()); if s<1e-300: w=np.full(m,1.0/m); else w/=s
    return w
```
This might over-smooth; use one as init.

```
def search_support_candidates(N, stop_time, rng, max_opt=4):
    cands=[]
    ms=candidate_m_sizes(N)
    if len(ms)>8: ms=ms[::2]
    pool=[]
    for m in ms:
       if time.monotonic() > stop_time: break
       for rep in range(3):
          supp=greedy_support(N,m,rng)
          supp,n_eff=normalize_support(supp)
          if supp.size>=10 and n_eff <= 10000: pool.append((n_eff,supp))
          if time>stop: break
       supp=np.sort(rng.choice(N,size=m,replace=False).astype(np.int64))
       supp,n_eff=normalize_support(supp)
       if supp.size>=10 and n_eff <=10000: pool.append((n_eff,supp))
    # score pool by equal score with n_eff
    scored=[]
    for n_eff,supp in pool:
       sc,maxc=support_equal_score(supp,n_eff)
       # maybe score with 2*n_eff*maxc*(1+0? equal weights order counts) yes.
       scored.append((sc,supp,maxc))
    if not scored: return []
    scores=np.array([x[0] for x in scored])
    order=np.argsort(scores)
    R=10
    top=[scored[i] for i in order[:R]]
    refined=[]
    for sc,supp,maxc in top:
       if time.monotonic()>stop_time: break
       # refine support in box maybe n=supp.max()+1 (n_eff)
       n_box=int(supp.max())+1 if supp.size else 1
       supp_ref,_,_,_=refine_support_counts(supp,n_box,iters=300,cand_pool=20,rng=rng)
       supp_ref,n_ref=normalize_support(supp_ref)
       if supp_ref.size<10: continue
       sc_ref,mc=support_equal_score(supp_ref,n_ref)
       refined.append((sc_ref,supp_ref,mc))
    opts=top+refined
    oscores=np.array([x[0] for x in opts])
   oorder=np.argsort(oscores)
    # unique supports key tuple
    seen=set(); out=[]
    limit=min(max_opt, len(opts))
    for i in oorder:
       if len(out)>=limit: break
       sc,supp,mc=opts[int(i)]
       key=tuple(supp.tolist())
       if key in seen: continue
       seen.add(key)
       # optimize weights
       w0=support_heuristic_weights(supp,int(supp.max())+1, iters=20)
       if w0 is None or float(w0.sum())<1e-8:
           w0=np.full(supp.size,1.0/supp.size)
       w=np.copy(w0); w=w/ max(float(w.sum()),1e-12)
       best_w=w; best_sc=support_weight_score(supp,w,n_ref) # need n_ref? support tuple doesn't have n; use n_eff=supp.max()+1 after normalization
       n_eff=int(supp.max())+1
       m=supp.size
       maxfun = 1800 if m<=100 else 1200 if m<=180 else 800
       for beta in [20.0,100.0,500.0,2000.0]:
          if time.monotonic() > stop_time - 5: break
          w=run_support_lbfgs(w,supp,n_eff,beta,maxiter=500,maxfun=maxfun)
          scw=support_weight_score(supp,w,n_eff)
          if scw<best_sc:
             best_sc=scw; best_w=w.copy()
       a=support_to_array(supp,best_w,n_eff)
       out.append(a)
    return out
```

### Exploit parent support
```
def exploit_parent_supports(parent,n,stop_time,rng,max_cand=3):
   out=[]
   if parent is None: return out
   p=parent
   vals=p>1e-12
   support_all=np.where(vals)[0]
   if support_all.size==0: return out
   # If dense, using top m may not be best but okay.
   for m in [30,50,80,110,140,180,220]:
      if time>stop: break
      if m>support_all.size: m=support_all.size
      if m<10: break
      idx=np.argsort(p)[::-1][:m]
      supp,neff=normalize_support(idx)
      if supp.size<10 or neff>10000: continue
      w=p[supp]
      S=w.sum()
      if S<1e-8: w=np.full(supp.size,1.0/supp.size)
      else: w=w/S
      # Optional heuristic blend with inverse? Not.
      best_w=w; n_eff=neff
      best_sc=support_weight_score(supp,best_w,n_eff)
      w_eq=np.full(supp.size,1.0/w.size)
      w_init=max? We'll run L-BFGS from w then w_eq maybe.
      maxfun = 1200
      for start_w in [w,w_eq]:
         ww=np.copy(start_w); ww/=max(ww.sum(),1e-12)
         for beta in [50.0,300.0,1500.0]:
            if time>stop: break
            ww=run_support_lbfgs(ww,supp,n_eff,beta,maxiter=500,maxfun=maxfun)
            sc=support_weight_score(supp,ww,n_eff)
            if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best_w=ww.copy()
      a=support_to_array(supp,best_w,n_eff)
      out.append(a)
   return out
```

   if time>deadline-60: break

# If no finite, choose best internal

```python
"""
Hybrid search for low linear self-convolution peaks.

The function minimize score = 2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a)^2) for a >= 0.
The search combines:
  * combinatorial support construction (sum-multiplicity-aware greedy + local search),
  * exact LSE/L-BFGS weight optimization on fixed sparse supports,
  * full-length LSE/L-BFGS polishing,
  * parent exploitation and random/perturbation local search.

The final answer is selected using the supplied black-box evaluate_sequence when
possible, with an internal FFT-based scorer as a fast proxy.
"""
import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize


# -----------------------------
# Global helpers for L-BFGS wrappers (no nested closures / lambdas)
# -----------------------------

_SUP_N = 0
_SUP_SUMS = None
_SUP_SUMS_R = None
_SUP_BETA = 1.0

_FULL_BETA = 1.0


# -----------------------------
# Basic utilities
# -----------------------------

def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def clean_array(a, to_sum=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    else:
        a = (a / s) * to_sum
    return a


def trim_small(a, eps=1e-14):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    mask = a > eps
    if not np.any(mask):
        return a
    idx = np.where(mask)[0]
    first = int(idx[0])
    last = int(idx[-1])
    if first > 0 or last < a.size - 1:
        a = a[first:last + 1]
    return a


def make_uniform(n):
    n = max(1, int(n))
    return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n


def interpolate_to_n(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    if a.size == n:
        return clean_array(a)
    if a.size < 2:
        return clean_array(np.ones(n) * (float(a.sum()) / max(1, a.size)))
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return clean_array(out)


def load_parent_array():
    try:
        h = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821
        if h is None:
            return None
        a = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if a.size < 16 or s < 0.01:
            return None
        return a / s
    except Exception:
        return None


def make_cosine(n, amp=0.35, phase=0.0):
    n = max(2, int(n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    a = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x + phase)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-4)
    return clean_array(a)


def make_smooth_fourier(n, rng):
    n = max(16, int(n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(1, 12):
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.04, 0.22))
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, 4 * k)))
        a += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * freq * x + phase)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-4)
    return clean_array(a)


def normalize_support(supp):
    supp = np.asarray(supp, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    if supp.size == 0:
        return supp, 1
    mn = int(supp.min())
    supp = supp - mn
    supp = np.unique(supp)
    if supp.size == 0:
        return supp, 1
    return supp, int(supp.max()) + 1


# -----------------------------
# FFT convolution / full-score helpers
# -----------------------------

def fast_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    m = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, m)[:2 * n - 1]
    return np.real(c)


def score_full_internal(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if n <= 0 or s < 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    c = fast_conv(a)
    mx = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mx / (s * s))


def lse_linear_score_grad(a, beta):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(0)
    c = fast_conv(a)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-10:
        return 1e12, np.zeros(n)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(n)
    beta = float(beta)
    scaled = beta * (c - cmax)
    max_scaled = float(scaled.max())
    e = np.exp(scaled - max_scaled)
    z = float(e.sum())
    p = e / z
    lse = cmax + (max_scaled + float(np.log(z))) / beta

    m2 = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    ra = a[::-1].copy()
    RA = np.fft.rfft(ra, m2)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, m2)
    h = np.fft.irfft(RA * P, m2)[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    h = np.real(h)

    s2 = s * s
    s3 = s2 * s
    score = 2.0 * n * lse / s2
    grad = (2.0 * n / s3) * (2.0 * h * s - 2.0 * lse)
    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return float(score), grad


# -----------------------------
# Full L-BFGS wrapper
# -----------------------------

def set_full_beta(beta):
    global _FULL_BETA
    _FULL_BETA = float(beta)


def full_lbfgs_fun(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    f, g = lse_linear_score_grad(x, _FULL_BETA)
    s = float(x.sum())
    reg = 1e-10 * (s - 1.0) * (s - 1.0)
    g = g + 2e-10 * (s - 1.0)
    g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return float(f + reg), g


def run_full_lbfgs(a0, beta, maxiter, maxfun):
    a0 = clean_array(a0)
    n = int(a0.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a0
    set_full_beta(beta)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1000.0)] * n
    opts = {
        "maxiter": int(maxiter),
        "maxfun": int(maxfun),
        "ftol": 1e-14,
        "gtol": 1e-10,
        "maxls": 20,
    }
    try:
        res = minimize(full_lbfgs_fun, a0, method="L-BFGS-B", bounds=bounds, options=opts)
        x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        x = a0
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s < 1e-12:
        return a0
    return x / s


# -----------------------------
# Support-wrapped LSE / L-BFGS
# -----------------------------

def set_support_global(supp, n, beta):
    global _SUP_N, _SUP_SUMS, _SUP_SUMS_R, _SUP_BETA
    s = np.asarray(supp, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    _SUP_N = int(n)
    _SUP_SUMS = s[:, None] + s[None, :]
    _SUP_SUMS_R = _SUP_SUMS.ravel()
    _SUP_BETA = float(beta)


def support_lbfgs_fun(x):
    w = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(w.size)
    if m == 0:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(0)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s < 1e-10:
        return 1e12, np.zeros(m)

    outer = w[:, None] * w[None, :]
    c = np.bincount(_SUP_SUMS_R, weights=outer.ravel(), minlength=2 * _SUP_N - 1)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(m)

    beta = float(_SUP_BETA)
    scaled = beta * (c - cmax)
    max_scaled = float(scaled.max())
    e = np.exp(scaled - max_scaled)
    z = float(e.sum())
    p = e / z
    lse = cmax + (max_scaled + float(np.log(z))) / beta

    D = p[_SUP_SUMS]
    grad_lse = 2.0 * (D @ w)
    s2 = s * s
    s3 = s2 * s
    score = 2.0 * _SUP_N * lse / s2
    grad = (2.0 * _SUP_N / s3) * (grad_lse * s - 2.0 * lse)

    reg = 1e-10 * (s - 1.0) * (s - 1.0)
    grad = grad + 2e-10 * (s - 1.0)
    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return float(score + reg), grad


def run_support_lbfgs(w0, supp, n, beta, maxiter, maxfun):
    w0 = np.asarray(w0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(w0.size)
    if m == 0:
        return w0
    s0 = float(w0.sum())
    if s0 < 1e-10 or not np.any(w0 > 0.0):
        w0 = np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w0 = w0 / s0

    set_support_global(supp, n, beta)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1000.0)] * m
    opts = {
        "maxiter": int(maxiter),
        "maxfun": int(maxfun),
        "ftol": 1e-14,
        "gtol": 1e-10,
        "maxls": 20,
    }
    try:
        res = minimize(support_lbfgs_fun, w0, method="L-BFGS-B", bounds=bounds, options=opts)
        w = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        w = w0
    w = np.nan_to_num(w, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    w = np.clip(w, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s < 1e-10:
        return w0
    return w / s


def support_weight_score(supp, w, n):
    supp = np.asarray(supp, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(supp.size)
    if m == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s < 1e-10:
        return np.inf
    sums = (supp[:, None] + supp[None, :]).ravel()
    outer = (w[:, None] * w[None, :]).ravel()
    c = np.bincount(sums, weights=outer, minlength=2 * int(n) - 1)
    mx = float(c.max())
    return float(2.0 * int(n) * mx / (s * s))


def support_to_array(supp, w, n):
    supp = np.asarray(supp, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if supp.size == 0:
        a[0] = 1.0
        return a
    a[supp] = w
    return clean_array(a)


def support_heuristic_weights(supp, n, iters=25):
    supp = np.asarray(supp, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    m = int(supp.size)
    if m == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)
    sums = supp[:, None] + supp[None, :]
    for _ in range(int(iters)):
        outer = w[:, None] * w[None, :]
        cnt = np.bincount(sums.ravel(), weights=outer.ravel(), minlength=max(1, 2 * int(n) - 1))
        tot = float(cnt.sum())
        if tot < 1e-300:
            break
        p = cnt / tot
        D = p[sums]
        g = D @ w + 1e-12
        w2 = w / g
        s2 = float(w2.sum())
        if s2 < 1e-300:
            break
        w2 /= s2
        w = 0.5 * w + 0.5 * w2
        w = np.clip(w, 1e-12, None)
        s = float(w.sum())
        if s < 1e-300:
            w = np.full(m, 1.0 / m, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w /= s
    return w


# -----------------------------
# Support construction / refinement
# -----------------------------

def greedy_support(n, m, rng, pool_size=140):
    n = int(n)
    m = int(m)
    if n <= 0 or m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.int64)
    if m >= n:
        return np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)

    used = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
    counts = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.int64)

    first = int(rng.integers(n))
    used[first] = True
    existing = np.array([first], dtype=np.int64)
    counts[2 * first] += 1

    for _ in range(m - 1):
        cands = []
        attempts = 0
        while len(cands) < pool_size and attempts < pool_size * 25:
            attempts += 1
            x = int(rng.integers(n))
            if not used[x]:
                cands.append(x)
        if not cands:
            break
        cand = np.asarray(cands, dtype=np.int64)
        if existing.size == 0:
            x = int(cand[0])
        else:
            sums = cand[:, None] + existing[None, :]
            new_counts = counts[sums] + 2
            loop_counts = counts[2 * cand] + 1
            max_new = np.maximum(new_counts.max(axis=1), loop_counts)
            nf = new_counts.astype(np.float64)
            lf = loop_counts.astype(np.float64)
            l2 = np.sum(nf * nf, axis=1) + lf * lf
            order = np.lexsort((l2, max_new))
            x = int(cand[int(order[0])])

        used[x] = True
        old = existing
        np.add.at(counts, old + x, 2)
        counts[2 * x] += 1
        existing = np.append(existing, x)

    return existing


def support_equal_score(supp, n):
    supp = np.asarray(supp, dtype=np.int64).ravel()
    m = int(supp.size)
    if m == 0:
        return np.inf, 0
    n = int(n)
    sums = (supp[:, None] + supp[None, :]).ravel()
    cnt = np.bincount(sums, minlength=max(1, 2 * n - 1))
    mx = int(cnt.max())
    sc = float(2.0 * n * mx / (m * m))
    return sc, mx


def refine_support_counts(supp, n, iters=300, cand_pool=18, rng=None):
    supp = np.asarray(supp, dtype=np.int64).ravel().copy()
    m = int(supp.size)
    n = int(n)
    if m == 0:
        counts = np.zeros(max(1, 2 * n - 1), dtype=np.int64)
        return supp, counts, 0, 0
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)

    used = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
    used[supp] = True
    sums = (supp[:, None] + supp[None, :]).ravel()
    counts = np.bincount(sums, minlength=max(1, 2 * n - 1)).astype(np.int64)

    maxc = int(counts.max())
    sumsq = int(np.dot(counts, counts))
    cur_n_eff = int(supp.max() - supp.min() + 1) if m > 1 else 1

    def candidate_new_n(others, x):
        if others.size == 0:
            return 1
        return int(max(x, int(others.max())) - min(x, int(others.min())) + 1)

    for _ in range(int(iters)):
        idx = int(rng.integers(m))
        old = int(supp[idx])

        inc_old = np.full(m, 2, dtype=np.int64)
        inc_old[idx] = 1
        old_sums = old + supp
        np.add.at(counts, old_sums, -inc_old)

        max_no = int(counts.max())
        sumsq_no = int(np.dot(counts, counts))
        others = np.delete(supp, idx)

        current_cost1 = int(cur_n_eff * maxc)
        current_cost2 = int(sumsq)

        best_x = -1
        best_max = 0
        best_sumsq = 0
        best_cost1 = current_cost1
        best_cost2 = current_cost2

        cands = []
        attempts = 0
        while len(cands) < cand_pool and attempts < cand_pool * 25:
            attempts += 1
            x = int(rng.integers(n))
            if not used[x] and x != old:
                cands.append(x)

        if cands and others.size > 0:
            cand_arr = np.asarray(cands, dtype=np.int64)
            all_sums = cand_arr[:, None] + others[None, :]
            vals = counts[all_sums]
            off_max = vals.max(axis=1) + 2
            loop_sums = 2 * cand_arr
            loop_vals = counts[loop_sums]
            cand_max = np.maximum(np.maximum(int(max_no), off_max), loop_vals + 1)
            sq_inc = np.sum(4 * vals.astype(np.int64) + 4, axis=1) + (2 * loop_vals + 1)
            cand_sumsq = sumsq_no + sq_inc
            new_n = np.array([candidate_new_n(others, int(x)) for x in cand_arr], dtype=np.int64)
            cost1 = new_n * cand_max
            cost2 = cand_sumsq

            better = (cost1 < best_cost1) | ((cost1 == best_cost1) & (cost2 < best_cost2))
            if np.any(better):
                choices = np.where(better)[0]
                pick = int(rng.integers(choices.size))
                bi = int(choices[pick])
                best_x = int(cand_arr[bi])
                best_max = int(cand_max[bi])
                best_sumsq = int(cand_sumsq[bi])
                best_cost1 = int(cost1[bi])
                best_cost2 = int(cost2[bi])

        if best_x != -1 and (best_cost1 < current_cost1 or (best_cost1 == current_cost1 and best_cost2 < current_cost2)):
            if others.size:
                np.add.at(counts, best_x + others, 2)
            counts[2 * best_x] += 1
            used[old] = False
            used[best_x] = True
            supp[idx] = best_x
            maxc = int(counts.max())
            sumsq = int(np.dot(counts, counts))
            cur_n_eff = int(supp.max() - supp.min() + 1) if m > 1 else 1
        else:
            np.add.at(counts, old_sums, inc_old)
            # state restored; maxc/sumsq/cur_n_eff unchanged

    # Final authoritative recount (and normalization is done by caller)
    sums = (supp[:, None] + supp[None, :]).ravel()
    counts = np.bincount(sums, minlength=max(1, 2 * n - 1)).astype(np.int64)
    maxc = int(counts.max())
    sumsq = int(np.dot(counts, counts))
    return supp, counts, maxc, sumsq


def candidate_m_sizes(n):
    n = max(1, int(n))
    base = float(np.sqrt(n))
    raw = []
    for c in (0.8, 1.0, 1.2, 1.4, 1.6, 1.8, 2.0, 2.3, 2.6, 3.0):
        raw.append(int(base * c + 0.5))
    raw.append(int(np.sqrt(8.0 * n / 1.5) + 0.5))
    raw.append(int(np.sqrt(12.0 * n / 1.5) + 0.5))
    ms = set()
    for x in raw:
        x = int(x)
        if x < 24:
            x = 24
        if x > 260:
            x = 260
        ms.add(x)
    out = sorted(ms)
    if len(out) > 7:
        out = out[::2]
        if out[-1] != max(ms):
            out.append(int(max(ms)))
    return out


def search_support_candidates(N, stop_time, rng, max_opt=4):
    N = int(N)
    out = []
    if N <= 0:
        return out

    ms = candidate_m_sizes(N)
    pool = []

    # Greedy + random starts.
    for m in ms:
        if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
            break
        for rep in range(3):
            supp = greedy_support(N, m, rng)
            supp, neff = normalize_support(supp)
            if supp.size >= 12 and 1 <= neff <= 10000:
                pool.append((neff, supp))
            if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
                break
        if m <= N:
            supp = np.sort(rng.choice(N, size=m, replace=False).astype(np.int64))
            supp, neff = normalize_support(supp)
            if supp.size >= 12 and 1 <= neff <= 10000:
                pool.append((neff, supp))

    if not pool:
        return out

    scored = []
    for neff, supp in pool:
        sc, mx = support_equal_score(supp, neff)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, supp, mx))

    if not scored:
        return out

    sc_arr = np.array([x[0] for x in scored], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(sc_arr)
    top = [scored[int(i)] for i in order[:min(10, len(scored))]]

    refined = []
    for sc, supp, mx in top:
        if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
            break
        n_box = int(supp.max()) + 1 if supp.size else 1
        supp_ref, _, _, _ = refine_support_counts(supp, n_box, iters=300, cand_pool=18, rng=rng)
        supp_ref, neff_ref = normalize_support(supp_ref)
        if supp_ref.size < 12:
            continue
        sc_ref, mx_ref = support_equal_score(supp_ref, neff_ref)
        if np.isfinite(sc_ref):
            refined.append((sc_ref, supp_ref, mx_ref))

    opts = top + refined
    osc_arr = np.array([x[0] for x in opts], dtype=np.float64)
    oorder = np.argsort(osc_arr)

    seen = set()
    limit = int(max_opt)
    for ii in oorder:
        if len(out) >= limit:
            break
        sc, supp, mx = opts[int(ii)]
        supp, neff = normalize_support(supp)
        if supp.size < 12 or neff > 10000:
            continue
        key = tuple(int(x) for x in supp)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)

        w = support_heuristic_weights(supp, neff, iters=18)
        sw = float(w.sum())
        if w is None or not np.isfinite(sw) or sw < 1e-8:
            w = np.full(supp.size, 1.0 / supp.size, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w = w / sw

        best_w = w.copy()
        best_sc = support_weight_score(supp, best_w, neff)

        m = int(supp.size)
        if m <= 100:
            maxfun = 1600
        elif m <= 170:
            maxfun = 1100
        else:
            maxfun = 700

        for beta in (20.0, 100.0, 500.0, 2000.0):
            if time.monotonic() > stop_time - 3.0:
                break
            w = run_support_lbfgs(w, supp, neff, beta, maxiter=450, maxfun=maxfun)
            scw = support_weight_score(supp, w, neff)
            if np.isfinite(scw) and scw < best_sc:
                best_sc = scw
                best_w = w.copy()

        a = support_to_array(supp, best_w, neff)
        a = trim_small(a, 1e-14)
        out.append(a)

    return out


# -----------------------------
# Candidate recording
# -----------------------------

def record_candidate(cands, a):
    a = trim_small(clean_array(a), 1e-14)
    sc = score_full_internal(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        sc = 1e100
    cands.append((float(sc), a))
    return float(sc), a


def best_internal(cands):
    if not cands:
        return np.inf, make_uniform(64)
    sc_arr = np.array([x[0] for x in cands], dtype=np.float64)
    i = int(np.argmin(sc_arr))
    return float(cands[i][0]), cands[i][1].copy()


# -----------------------------
# Parent exploitation
# -----------------------------

def exploit_parent_supports(parent, stop_time, max_cand=3):
    out = []
    if parent is None:
        return out
    p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    nz = np.where(p > 1e-12)[0]
    if nz.size < 12:
        return out

    for m in (40, 70, 100, 130, 170, 210, 250):
        if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
            break
        if m > nz.size:
            m = int(nz.size)
        if m < 12:
            break
        idx = np.argsort(p)[::-1][:m]
        supp, neff = normalize_support(idx)
        if supp.size < 12 or neff > 10000:
            continue

        w = p[supp].astype(np.float64)
        sw = float(w.sum())
        if sw < 1e-10:
            w = np.full(supp.size, 1.0 / supp.size, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w = w / sw

        best_w = w.copy()
        best_sc = support_weight_score(supp, best_w, neff)

        w_eq = np.full(supp.size, 1.0 / supp.size, dtype=np.float64)
        starts = [w, w_eq]
        m = int(supp.size)
        maxfun = 1000 if m <= 120 else 700

        for st in starts:
            ww = np.asarray(st, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            sww = float(ww.sum())
            if sww < 1e-10:
                ww = w_eq.copy()
            else:
                ww = ww / sww
            for beta in (50.0, 300.0, 1500.0):
                if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
                    break
                ww = run_support_lbfgs(ww, supp, neff, beta, maxiter=450, maxfun=maxfun)
                sc = support_weight_score(supp, ww, neff)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc
                    best_w = ww.copy()

        a = support_to_array(supp, best_w, neff)
        a = trim_small(a, 1e-14)
        out.append(a)
        if len(out) >= int(max_cand):
            break
    return out


# -----------------------------
# Oracle wrapper
# -----------------------------

def safe_oracle(a):
    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if a.size == 0:
            return np.inf
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s < 0.01:
            return np.inf
        seq = [float(x) for x in a]
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)  # noqa: F821
        val = float(val)
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            return np.inf
        return val
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


# -----------------------------
# Main proposer
# -----------------------------

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing the given evaluator."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + total - 90.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    parent = load_parent_array()

    if parent is not None and 300 <= int(parent.size) <= 10000:
        n_primary = int(parent.size)
    else:
        n_primary = 6962
    n_primary = int(np.clip(n_primary, 512, 10000))

    cands = []

    # Basic robust candidates.
    record_candidate(cands, make_uniform(n_primary))
    record_candidate(cands, make_cosine(n_primary, 0.35, 0.0))
    record_candidate(cands, make_cosine(n_primary, 0.55, 1.1))
    record_candidate(cands, make_smooth_fourier(n_primary, rng))

    if parent is not None:
        a_parent = interpolate_to_n(parent, n_primary)
        record_candidate(cands, a_parent)
        a_parent_small = trim_small(parent, 1e-14)
        if a_parent_small.size >= 32 and a_parent_small.size <= 10000:
            record_candidate(cands, a_parent_small)

    # Quick parent support exploitation (cheap relative to support search).
    parent_stop = min(deadline - 600.0, t0 + 0.06 * total)
    for a in exploit_parent_supports(parent, parent_stop, max_cand=3):
        record_candidate(cands, a)

    # Primary support-based search.
    supp_stop_primary = min(deadline - 300.0, t0 + 0.55 * total)
    supp_cands = search_support_candidates(n_primary, supp_stop_primary, rng, max_opt=5)
    for a in supp_cands:
        record_candidate(cands, a)

    # If time remains, explore a slightly longer length (score has explicit n factor,
    # but better support ratios can win).
    n_alt = int(min(10000, max(n_primary + 800, 9000)))
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 420.0 and abs(n_alt - n_primary) > 400:
        supp_stop_alt = min(deadline - 240.0, t0 + 0.72 * total)
        supp_cands_alt = search_support_candidates(n_alt, supp_stop_alt, rng, max_opt=3)
        for a in supp_cands_alt:
            record_candidate(cands, a)

    # Full L-BFGS polish from the best internal starts.
    internal_best_sc, internal_best_a = best_internal(cands)
    print("Internal best before full polish: %.8f (n=%d)" % (internal_best_sc, internal_best_a.size), flush=True)

    starts = []
    # Top few internal candidates of primary length (or all if fewer).
    primary_cands = []
    for sc, a in cands:
        if a.size == n_primary:
            primary_cands.append((sc, a))
    if primary_cands:
        p_arr = np.array([x[0] for x in primary_cands], dtype=np.float64)
        p_order = np.argsort(p_arr)
        for ii in p_order[:3]:
            starts.append(primary_cands[int(ii)][1].copy())
    if parent is not None:
        starts.append(interpolate_to_n(parent, n_primary))
    starts.append(internal_best_a.copy())
    starts.append(make_uniform(n_primary))

    # De-duplicate starts by tuple of rounded values (rough, low cost).
    cleaned_starts = []
    seen_starts = set()
    for a in starts:
        if a.size != n_primary:
            continue
        key = tuple(float(x) for x in np.round(a, 8))
        if key in seen_starts:
            continue
        seen_starts.add(key)
        cleaned_starts.append(a)
        if len(cleaned_starts) >= 3:
            break

    # Time one gradient to choose maxfun.
    if cleaned_starts:
        test_a = clean_array(cleaned_starts[0])
        tg = time.monotonic()
        try:
            lse_linear_score_grad(test_a, 50.0)
            perf = max(1e-4, time.monotonic() - tg)
        except Exception:
            perf = 0.005
    else:
        perf = 0.005

    for st in cleaned_starts:
        stop_full = min(deadline - 180.0, t0 + 0.85 * total)
        if time.monotonic() > stop_full:
            break
        a_cur = clean_array(st)
        a_cur = trim_small(a_cur, 1e-14)
        for beta in (20.0, 100.0, 400.0, 1500.0):
            if time.monotonic() > stop_full:
                break
            remaining = max(60.0, stop_full - time.monotonic())
            maxfun = int(500 + remaining / max(perf, 1e-4) * 0.25)
            maxfun = int(np.clip(maxfun, 300, 1800))
            a2 = run_full_lbfgs(a_cur, beta, maxiter=600, maxfun=maxfun)
            a2 = trim_small(a2, 1e-14)
            sc2, a2 = record_candidate(cands, a2)
            if sc2 < score_full_internal(a_cur) - 1e-8:
                a_cur = a2
                internal_best_sc, internal_best_a = best_internal(cands)
                print("Full polish improved: %.8f (n=%d, beta=%g)" % (internal_best_sc, internal_best_a.size, beta), flush=True)

    # Oracle evaluation of top internal candidates.
    print("Selecting best by black-box evaluate_sequence ...", flush=True)
    sc_arr = np.array([x[0] for x in cands], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(sc_arr)

    best_oracle = np.inf
    best_oracle_internal = np.inf
    best_a = None

    evaluated = 0
    for ii in order:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 45.0:
            break
        if evaluated >= 45:
            break
        i = int(ii)
        int_sc, a = cands[i]
        a = trim_small(clean_array(a), 1e-14)
        if a.size < 1 or a.size > 12000:
            continue
        osc = safe_oracle(a)
        evaluated += 1
        if np.isfinite(osc) and osc < best_oracle:
            best_oracle = float(osc)
            best_oracle_internal = float(int_sc)
            best_a = a.copy()
            print(
                "NEW BEST oracle=%.10f internal=%.10f n=%d t=%.0fs evals=%d" %
                (best_oracle, best_oracle_internal, best_a.size, time.monotonic() - t0, evaluated),
                flush=True
            )

    if best_a is None:
        if internal_best_sc < np.inf:
            best_a = internal_best_a.copy()
        else:
            best_a = make_uniform(n_primary)

    # Mild randomized refinement around the oracle-best candidate.
    base = best_a.astype(np.float64).copy()
    for it in range(30):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 15.0:
            break
        strength = 0.008 + 0.035 * (float(it) / 29.0)
        a2 = base * (1.0 + rng.normal(0.0, strength, size=base.size))
        a2 = clean_array(a2)
        a2 = trim_small(a2, 1e-14)
        osc2 = safe_oracle(a2)
        if np.isfinite(osc2) and osc2 < best_oracle:
            best_oracle = float(osc2)
            best_a = a2
            print("PERTURB BEST oracle=%.10f n=%d" % (best_oracle, best_a.size), flush=True)

    # Final sanitation.
    best_a = np.asarray(best_a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    best_a = np.where(np.isfinite(best_a), best_a, 0.0)
    best_a = np.clip(best_a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(best_a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        best_a = make_uniform(n_primary)
    else:
        best_a = best_a / s

    out = [float(x) for x in best_a]

    # Optional final oracle check (helps logging; safe if already called before).
    try:
        final_oracle = safe_oracle(out)
    except Exception:
        final_oracle = np.nan
    print("Final length=%d, final internal=%.10f, final oracle=%.10f, elapsed=%.1fs" %
          (len(out), score_full_internal(best_a), final_oracle, time.monotonic() - t0), flush=True)

    return out
```
